⏺ I checked D&D Beyond and found three free sets of rules documentation:

  1. Basic Rules (2024): https://www.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/free-rules
     - The current core rules, published Sep 1, 2024.
     - Players: how to play, character creation, all 12 classes, backgrounds and
       species, feats, equipment, spells, a rules glossary and a character
       sheet.
     - DMs: running sessions, a toolbox (traps, hazards, travel, curses), and
       magic items A–Z.
     - Monsters: how stat blocks work, plus 300+ monsters.
  2. Basic Rules (2014): https://www.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/basic-rules-2014
     - The older 5e rules: character creation, how to play, magic, DM tools, and
       appendices on conditions, gods, factions and planes.
  3. SRD (System Reference Document): https://www.dndbeyond.com/srd
     - v5.2.1 (May 2025) covers the 2024 rules. It's licensed CC-BY-4.0, which
       can't be revoked, and comes in EN/DE/ES/FR/IT with a guide for converting
       from v5.1.
     - v5.1 covers the 2014 rules under OGL 1.0a and CC-BY-4.0. It's marked
       legacy.
     - The SRD is the free rules text you can reuse to make your own compatible
       products or tools.

  The Player's Handbook, Dungeon Master's Guide and Monster Manual aren't free;
  you have to buy them.

  What are you after: a specific rule, character building, DM material, or
  something like scraping the SRD for a tool?

⏺ I built 12 level-1 characters from the D&D Beyond 2014 Basic Rules. I took
  every class feature, spell list, gear option and racial trait from the site's
  own pages, not from memory.

  How I kept them balanced (my choices):
  - Everyone uses the same standard array (15/14/13/12/10/8), so nobody gets a
    better roll.
  - Everyone has max HP and only the starting gear from their class and
    background.
  - Each character's main stat comes out at +3.
  - The site has 12 race/subrace options, and each is used once. Each of the 6
    backgrounds is used twice.
  - Sleep is the strongest level-1 spell, so only the wizard has it.
  - The site has only one subclass per class. Only Cleric (Life), Sorcerer
    (Draconic) and Warlock (Fiend) pick theirs at level 1.

  ---

  1. Barbarian: Ghorza Ironjaw (Half-Orc, Folk Hero)
  STR 17 DEX 14 CON 14 INT 8 WIS 12 CHA 10 · HP 14 · AC 14 (no armor) · Speed 30
  - Saves: Str +5, Con +4
  - Skills: Athletics +5, Perception +3, Animal Handling +3, Survival +3,
    Intimidation +2
  - Attacks: Greataxe +5, 1d12+3 (+2 while raging). 2 handaxes and 4 javelins,
    thrown, +5, 1d6+3
  - Features: Rage 2 per day (resists physical damage), Unarmored Defense,
    Relentless Endurance (drop to 1 HP instead of 0, once per day), Savage
    Attacks (extra die on crits), darkvision
  - Gear: explorer's pack, smith's tools, shovel, iron pot, 10 gp
  - Languages: Common, Orc
  - Fun: Rage plus dropping to 1 HP instead of 0 makes her the hardest character
    to kill.

  2. Bard: Lyra Vance (Half-Elf, Soldier: an army drummer turned battlefield
  poet)
  STR 8 DEX 14 CON 14 INT 10 WIS 13 CHA 17 · HP 10 · AC 13 (leather) · Speed 30
  - Saves: Dex +4, Cha +5
  - Skills: Intimidation, Performance, Persuasion, Deception +5; Perception,
    Insight +3; Athletics +1
  - Attacks: Rapier +4, 1d8+2. Dagger +4, 1d4+2
  - Spells (save DC 13, attack +5, 2 slots):
    - Cantrips: Vicious Mockery, Prestidigitation
    - Known: Healing Word, Faerie Fire, Heroism, Charm Person
  - Features: Bardic Inspiration d6, 3 per day; Fey Ancestry; darkvision
  - Instruments: drum, lute, horn
  - Languages: Common, Elvish, Dwarvish
  - Fun: The party's talker and support.

  3. Cleric (Life Domain): Hilda Ambermantle (Hill Dwarf, Acolyte)
  STR 13 DEX 8 CON 16 INT 10 WIS 16 CHA 12 · HP 12 · AC 18 (chain mail + shield)
  · Speed 25 (heavy armor doesn't slow dwarves)
  - Saves: Wis +5, Cha +3
  - Skills: Insight +5, Medicine +5, Persuasion +3, Religion +2
  - Attacks: Warhammer +3, 1d8+1. Light crossbow +1, 1d8-1. Sacred Flame (DC 13,
    1d8 radiant)
  - Spells (DC 13, 2 slots):
    - Cantrips: Sacred Flame, Guidance, Light
    - Prepared: Healing Word, Guiding Bolt, Shield of Faith, Command
    - Always prepared: Bless, Cure Wounds
  - Features: Disciple of Life adds +3 to every heal from a spell slot (Cure 
    Wounds heals 1d8+6); resists poison; darkvision
  - Tools: brewer's supplies
  - Languages: Common, Dwarvish, Celestial, Elvish
  - Fun: A healer who can also hold the front line.

  4. Druid: Sylvara Moss (Wood Elf, Sage)
  STR 10 DEX 15 CON 14 INT 12 WIS 16 CHA 8 · HP 10 · AC 15 (leather + wooden
  shield) · Speed 35
  - Saves: Int +3, Wis +5
  - Skills: Perception +5, Survival +5, Arcana +3, History +3, Nature +3
  - Attacks: Scimitar +4, 1d6+2. Produce Flame +5, 1d8 fire
  - Spells (DC 13, 2 slots):
    - Cantrips: Produce Flame, Guidance
    - Prepared: Entangle, Healing Word, Goodberry, Thunderwave
  - Features: Druidic language, Mask of the Wild (can hide in light cover), Fey
    Ancestry, darkvision
  - Tools: herbalism kit
  - Languages: Common, Elvish, Sylvan, Primordial
  - Fun: Controls the battlefield. Wild Shape comes at level 2.

  5. Fighter: Borin Stonefist (Mountain Dwarf, Soldier)
  STR 17 DEX 13 CON 16 INT 8 WIS 12 CHA 10 · HP 13 · AC 16 (chain mail) · Speed
  25
  - Saves: Str +5, Con +5
  - Skills: Athletics +5, Perception +3, Insight +3, Intimidation +2
  - Attacks: Greatsword +5, 2d6+3, rerolling 1s and 2s. Battleaxe +5, 1d8+3. 2
    handaxes, thrown, +5, 1d6+3
  - Features: Great Weapon Fighting, Second Wind (heal 1d10+1), resists poison,
    darkvision
  - Gear: dungeoneer's pack, 10 gp
  - Tools: smith's tools, dice
  - Languages: Common, Dwarvish
  - Fun: The biggest hits in the party.

  6. Monk: Pip Underbough (Stout Halfling, Acolyte)
  STR 12 DEX 17 CON 14 INT 10 WIS 14 CHA 8 · HP 10 · AC 15 (no armor) · Speed 25
  - Saves: Str +3, Dex +5
  - Skills: Acrobatics +5, Stealth +5, Insight +4, Religion +2
  - Attacks: Shortsword +5, 1d6+3, plus a bonus-action punch (+5, 1d4+3). 10
    darts +5, 1d4+3
  - Features: Martial Arts, Unarmored Defense, Lucky (reroll 1s), Brave, resists
    poison
  - Tools: calligrapher's supplies
  - Languages: Common, Halfling, Elvish, Dwarvish
  - Fun: Two attacks every turn. Ki and extra speed come at level 2.

  7. Paladin: Sorasha Silverscale (Dragonborn, silver ancestry, Noble)
  STR 17 DEX 10 CON 14 INT 8 WIS 12 CHA 14 · HP 12 · AC 18 (chain mail + shield)
  · Speed 30
  - Saves: Wis +3, Cha +4
  - Skills: Athletics +5, Persuasion +4, Intimidation +4, History +1
  - Attacks: Longsword +5, 1d8+3. 5 javelins +5, 1d6+3. Breath weapon: 15 ft
    cold cone, Con save DC 12, 2d6, once per short rest
  - Features: Divine Sense 3 per day, Lay on Hands (5 HP pool), resists cold
  - Gear: priest's pack, holy symbol, signet ring, 25 gp
  - Languages: Common, Draconic, Celestial
  - Fun: A noble shield-bearer with a breath attack. Spells and Smite come at
    level 2.

  8. Ranger: Aelar Thistledown (High Elf, Folk Hero)
  STR 12 DEX 17 CON 14 INT 11 WIS 13 CHA 8 · HP 12 · AC 14 (leather; scale mail
  gives 16 but hurts Stealth) · Speed 30
  - Saves: Str +3, Dex +5
  - Skills: Stealth +5, Athletics +3, Perception +3, Animal Handling +3,
    Survival +3, Nature +2
  - Attacks: Longbow +5, 1d8+3. Two shortswords +5, 1d6+3, with an off-hand hit
    for 1d6
  - Features: Favored Enemy (goblins and gnolls), Natural Explorer (forest),
    Minor Illusion cantrip from High Elf, darkvision
  - Tools: woodcarver's tools
  - Languages: Common, Elvish, Sylvan, Goblin
  - Fun: The tracker and archer.

  9. Rogue: Wren Goodbarrel (Lightfoot Halfling, Criminal)
  STR 8 DEX 17 CON 14 INT 10 WIS 12 CHA 14 · HP 10 · AC 14 (leather) · Speed 25
  - Saves: Dex +5, Int +2
  - Skills: Stealth +7 (Expertise), Acrobatics +5, Sleight of Hand +5, Deception
    +4, Persuasion +4, Perception +3
  - Attacks: Rapier +5, 1d8+3. Shortbow +5, 1d6+3. 2 daggers +5, 1d4+3. Sneak
    Attack adds +1d6
  - Features: Expertise (Stealth, thieves' tools +7), Thieves' Cant, Lucky, can
    hide behind larger creatures
  - Tools: thieves' tools, disguise kit, cards. The disguise kit replaces a
    second set of thieves' tools, which the rules allow.
  - Languages: Common, Halfling
  - Fun: The scout and lockpick.

  10. Sorcerer (Draconic, red): Cassian Drake (Human, Noble)
  STR 9 DEX 14 CON 15 INT 11 WIS 13 CHA 16 · HP 9 · AC 15 (dragon scales) ·
  Speed 30
  - Saves: Con +4, Cha +5
  - Skills: Persuasion +5, Intimidation +5, Arcana +2, History +2
  - Attacks: Fire Bolt +5, 1d10. Shocking Grasp +5, 1d8. Light crossbow +4,
    1d8+2
  - Spells (DC 13, 2 slots):
    - Cantrips: Fire Bolt, Shocking Grasp, Message, Light
    - Known: Burning Hands (3d6 cone), Shield
  - Features: Draconic Resilience (+1 HP, natural armor), speaks Draconic
  - Gear: arcane focus, explorer's pack, 2 daggers, 25 gp
  - Languages: Common, Draconic, Elvish, Dwarvish
  - Fun: The fire blaster. Sorcery points come at level 2.

  11. Warlock (Fiend): Mercy (Tiefling, Criminal)
  STR 8 DEX 14 CON 13 INT 11 WIS 12 CHA 17 · HP 9 · AC 13 (leather) · Speed 30
  - Saves: Wis +3, Cha +5
  - Skills: Deception +5, Intimidation +5, Stealth +4, Arcana +2
  - Attacks: Eldritch Blast +5, 1d10 force, 120 ft. Light crossbow +4, 1d8+2
  - Spells (DC 13, 1 slot that comes back on a short rest):
    - Cantrips: Eldritch Blast, Mage Hand, plus Thaumaturgy from race
    - Known: Command, Hellish Rebuke (2d10 fire)
  - Features: Dark One's Blessing (4 temp HP each time you drop an enemy),
    resists fire, darkvision
  - Tools: thieves' tools, dice
  - Languages: Common, Infernal
  - Fun: Made a deal with a devil to escape a debt.

  12. Wizard: Fizwick Nackle (Rock Gnome, Sage)
  STR 8 DEX 13 CON 15 INT 17 WIS 12 CHA 10 · HP 8 · AC 11 (14 with Mage Armor) ·
  Speed 25
  - Saves: Int +5, Wis +3
  - Skills: Arcana +5, History +5, Investigation +5, Insight +3
  - Attacks: Ray of Frost +5, 1d8 and slows the target
  - Spells (DC 13, 2 slots):
    - Cantrips: Ray of Frost, Mage Hand, Prestidigitation
    - Spellbook: Sleep, Magic Missile, Mage Armor, Shield, Find Familiar, Detect
      Magic
    - Prepared: the first four. Find Familiar and Detect Magic can be cast as
      rituals without preparing them.
  - Features: Arcane Recovery, Gnome Cunning (advantage on mental saves against
    magic), darkvision
  - Tools: tinker's tools
  - Languages: Common, Gnomish, Draconic, Elvish
  - Fun: The only character with Sleep, which can end a fight at level 1.

  ---

  Spread: HP ranges from 8 (Wizard) to 14 (Barbarian), and AC from 11 (Wizard)
  to 18 (Cleric, Paladin).

  Ranger, Paladin and Monk feel the thinnest at level 1, because their spells,
  Smite and Ki all arrive at level 2. If your table plays one-shots at level 1,
  give those players a heads-up.

  The names and backstory hooks are mine, not from the site.

  Want them as a printable doc or PDF, or should I make any changes (other
  spells, different races)?

⏺ I filled out all 12 sheets. They're in
  /Users/ngur/claude/dnd-character-sheets/ (01_Barbarian_Ghorza.pdf through
  12_Wizard_Fizwick.pdf). I couldn't attach them here because file sending is
  switched off in this session.

  Sheet used: the official Wizards of the Coast fillable 5e sheet (3 pages),
  from media.wizards.com. The fields stay editable, so you can still change
  things or track HP during play.

  What's filled in:
  - Page 1:
    - Stats, saves and skills, with the proficiency dots ticked
    - HP, AC, speed and attacks, plus notes on how each attack works
    - Gear, gold, proficiencies, languages and features
  - Page 2:
    - Appearance, backstory and allies
    - The background feature, in wording checked against the Basic Rules
  - Page 3:
    - For spellcasters: spell save DC, attack bonus, slots and spells. Prepared
      spells are ticked; the wizard's two ritual spells are left unticked
      because they can be cast without preparing.
    - The ranger gets a page 3 for the High Elf Minor Illusion cantrip (DC 10).
    - Barbarian, Fighter, Monk, Paladin and Rogue have a blank page 3, since
      they have no spells at level 1.
  - Personality traits, ideals, bonds and flaws are taken from each background's
    official tables.

  How I checked them:
  - A script works out every modifier, save, skill and passive Perception from
    the ability scores. They all match the numbers I gave you earlier.
  - I read all 1,333 filled fields back out of the 12 PDFs, and none differ from
    what I intended.
  - I rendered the pages and looked at the densest ones. No text is cut off.

  Things to know:
  - Three damage boxes print small (6.5pt) because "bludgeoning" is a long word:
    the Cleric's warhammer, the Monk's unarmed strike and the Wizard's
    quarterstaff.
  - To fit their boxes I shortened three entries: the druid's hair, the
    sorcerer's skin, and the ranger's off-hand weapon (now "Off-hand sword").
  - The names, backstories and looks are my own invention.

  Want me to also make one combined, print-ready PDF of all 12? It has to be a
  flattened (non-editable) copy, because the sheets share field names and would
  overwrite each other if merged as they are.

⏺ 42/42 pass. Saving a memory note so future sessions know this project exists

  Listed 1 directory, wrote 2 memories (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ The battle map program is built in ~/claude/dnd-battlemap, and all 42 tests
  pass. The suite passed 6 runs in a row. Run uv run battlemap in that folder to
  start it; it opens the map in your browser.

  What it does:
  - Grid: each square is 5 ft. Squares are named like a chessboard (A1 is
    top-left). Maps are saved automatically.
  - DM: creates, resizes and deletes maps; draws walls along grid lines; paints
    blocking obstacles and difficult terrain; adds PCs, enemies, NPCs and items
    in any size from tiny to gargantuan (a gargantuan creature fills 4×4
    squares). The DM can move anything, walking it along a legal path or placing
    it anywhere.
  - Player: sees the map, measures, and moves only their own character. While
    dragging, the player sees the path and its cost in feet; an illegal move is
    refused with the reason (for example "Can't end a move in Goblin's space").
  - Measuring: a ruler for distance, a rectangle for size, area and the tokens
    inside, and a radius showing every square within N ft.
  - Live sync: a change made anywhere, including by a Claude, appears
    immediately in every open browser.

  Movement rules come from the D&D Beyond 2014 Basic Rules:
  - Diagonals cost 5 ft.
  - A diagonal step can't cut a wall corner.
  - Difficult terrain and other creatures' squares cost double.
  - You can pass through a friendly creature but not a hostile one of similar
    size.
  - You can't end a move in another creature's square.
  - A 2×2 creature needs a 2-square gap to get through.

  I left out the Dungeon Master's Guide rule where diagonals alternate 5 and 10
  ft, because that book isn't on the site to check against.

  Claude access is through an MCP server whose role is fixed when it's
  registered, either DM or a named player. A player-Claude can't move anyone
  else's token. It can read the map as a text grid with a legend, and it starts
  the map server itself if it isn't running.

  Decisions to check, since they're mine and not from the rules:
  - PCs and enemies are hostile to each other; NPCs are hostile to no one.
  - Items never block movement, and tiny creatures fill a whole square.
  - When the DM drags a token, it walks if a legal path exists (so the cost is
    reported) and otherwise just gets placed.
  - The radius tool measures with the same grid distance as movement, so it
    covers a square block rather than a circle.

  Bugs the browser tests caught, now fixed:
  - An invisible "No map yet" layer sat over the map and swallowed every click.
  - A live update from another player or Claude wiped whatever the DM was typing
    in the token or map-settings form.

  The four extras you picked, plus playing from your own devices on the same
  Wi-Fi, are written down in ROADMAP.md, and I saved a memory note pointing to
  it.

  Nothing is committed yet. I'd split it into five commits: scaffold, rules
  engine, server and API, MCP server, then GUI and docs.

  Two things need your go-ahead:
  1. Commit in those five parts?
  2. Connect Claude: add the MCP servers to your Claude config? For example a DM
     one and one for Lyra:
  claude mcp add -s user battlemap-dm -- uv run --directory
  ~/claude/dnd-battlemap battlemap mcp --actor dm

⏺ I folded the lazy-start change into the existing commits.
  - Lazy start: the code and its test are now in "Add the MCP server and command
    line" (d79502a), whose message now describes it. The README line is in the
    docs commit (9e8e57a).
  - Checks: the final file tree is byte-for-byte what was in the working tree
    before the rewrite. All 6 commits are signed, and each passes its own tests
    at that point (17 → 28 → 35 → 42 → 42). The working tree is clean.

  Why one server per player: the server has to know who a Claude is, and the
  Claude can't be the one who says. If the character were a tool argument, a
  Claude playing Lyra could just pass "dm" or "Ghorza" and the role rules would
  mean nothing. An MCP server's settings are fixed by whoever launches the
  session, so I tied the identity to the server. One identity per server meant
  one server per player.

  They don't have to be registered for every session, though. Options:

  1. Per-session config (recommended). Nothing registered for players. You start
     a player Claude with its identity fixed at launch, using the claude 
     --mcp-config flag (I checked your version has it). I'd add a battlemap play
     "Lyra Vance" command that opens Claude already set up as Lyra, so there's
     no JSON to copy. Other sessions are untouched, and the rules stay enforced.
  2. One shared player server, character passed as a tool argument. One
     registration and the simplest setup, but it runs on trust: any Claude can
     act as anyone. That's fine if every player is your own cooperative Claude.
  3. One registered server per player (what I first proposed). It works, but
     adds an MCP server and its tools to every session you start.

  Want option 1? I'd build battlemap play, test it, and show it to you before
  committing. The DM server could work the same way instead of staying
  registered for every session, if you'd rather keep your work sessions clean
  too.

⏺ I built battlemap play; it's uncommitted, waiting for your go. 44/44 tests
  pass.

  Usage, from ~/claude/dnd-battlemap:
  uv run battlemap play "Lyra Vance"                # extra claude flags go 
  after --, e.g. -- --model sonnet
  It opens a Claude session already acting as Lyra. Nothing gets added to your
  Claude config, and your other sessions are unaffected. The DM server stays
  registered for every session, as before.

  Checked:
  - New tests check the exact claude command it builds, and that the server set
    up for that session is Lyra and gets refused when it tries DM actions.
  - The real launch, described above, works end to end.

  A correction to what I told you earlier. I said a player Claude "can't cheat".
  That's only true within the battlemap tools. There are no logins in v1: every
  request just states who it is acting as. So a Claude with shell access could
  talk to the map server directly and claim to be the DM. The roles keep honest
  players and Claudes in line, but they won't stop a determined cheat; real
  enforcement needs the per-player join codes already on the roadmap. I fixed
  the README's wording to say this.

  Possible next step (not built): hide the DM-only tools from player sessions,
  so Lyra's tool list shows only what she can actually use.

  Commit? I'd fold this into the existing commits like last time: play and its
  tests into "Add the MCP server and command line", the README changes into the
  docs commit, then re-sign and re-check every commit.

❯ I want to also add options to select certain obstacles like trees, rocks,     
  etc. Instead of just a black square                                           

⏺ Good idea. It fits the existing obstacle paint tool. Proposed ground rules:
  - Rules stay the same: movement still only cares whether a square is blocking
    or difficult. Each obstacle type maps to one of those two.
  - Model on: today's Obstacle and Difficult paint tools, turned into a palette.
  - Affected: the GUI palette and drawing, the MCP add_obstacles tool, the text
    map Claudes read, the API, and saved maps. Existing maps keep loading, with
    their plain blocking/difficult squares unchanged.

  The Basic Rules give examples to anchor the types. Difficult terrain: "Low
  furniture, rubble, undergrowth, steep stairs, snow, and shallow bogs". The
  corner rule names "a wall, large tree, or other terrain feature that fills its
  space". A few decisions are yours:

⏺ User answered Claude's questions:
  ⎿  · Which obstacle types do you want? → Blocking natural/built, Difficult 
     (rules' examples), Props, Water, "Also icons for players, enemies, npcs, 
     etc"
     · Should each type fix its movement effect? → Fixed per type (Recommended)
     · How should obstacles look on the map? → Drawn icons (Recommended)
     · Anything to include in this change beyond types + look? Unselected stays 
     out of scope. → Multi-square obstacles

⏺ Four groups are confirmed, each type with a fixed effect, drawn icons, and
  multi-square obstacles, with cover effects left out. I'm asking which "icons
  for players, enemies, npcs" you meant before designing the data model.

⏺ User answered Claude's questions:
  ⎿  · For token icons (PCs, enemies, NPCs, items), what do you mean? → Default 
     per kind + pick per token (Recommended)

⏺ Token kinds get default icons the DM can change per token, and obstacles come
  in 16 types with fixed effects that can span several squares. Old saved maps
  will keep loading unchanged, and I'm starting with the backend.

      38  - **Ruler** (R): drag between two squares to get the distance.
      39  - **Area** (A): drag a rectangle to get its size, area and the 
          tokens inside.
      40  - **Radius** (C): drag out from a square to get every square wi
          thin that distance.
      41 -- DM only: **Wall** (W) draws walls along grid lines; **Obstacl
         -e** (O) and **Difficult** (D) paint terrain; **Erase** (E) remo
         -ves walls and obstacles under the pointer. **Add token** in the
         - side panel places a PC, enemy, NPC or item; click a token to e
         -dit or delete it.                                              
      41 +- DM only: **Wall** (W) draws walls along grid lines. **Terrain
         +** (O) places obstacles: pick a type from the palette (tree, bo
         +ulder, pillar, statue, table, crate, barrel, deep water, rubble
         +, undergrowth, low furniture, steep stairs, snow, shallow bog, 
         +or plain blocking/difficult) and a size; at 1x1 drag to paint s
         +quares, bigger sizes place one obstacle covering W x H squares.
         + **Erase** (E) removes walls and whole obstacles under the poin
         +ter. **Add token** in the side panel places a PC, enemy, NPC or
         + item with an icon; click a token to edit it (name, kind, size,
         + icon, color, notes) or delete it.                             
      42 +- Tokens show an icon: by default a shield for PCs, a skull for

         + leaf, flame, music, paw, dragon, crown, potion, key, scroll an
         +d the rest).

⏺ Obstacle types, multi-square obstacles and token icons are built. It's
  uncommitted, waiting for your go. 53/53 tests pass, 3 runs in a row.

  Obstacles:
  - 16 types, each with a fixed effect:
    - Blocking: plain, tree, boulder, pillar, statue, table, crate, barrel, deep
      water.
    - Difficult: plain, rubble, undergrowth, low furniture, steep stairs, snow,
      shallow bog. These are the Basic Rules' own examples of difficult terrain.
  - Terrain tool (key O) replaces the old Obstacle and Difficult buttons. It has
    a palette of drawn swatches and a W×H size. At 1×1 you drag to paint
    squares; bigger sizes place one object, such as a 2×2 tree or a 3×1 table.
  - A new obstacle replaces anything it overlaps, and erasing any square of an
    object removes all of it.
  - The corner rule applies to every blocking type, so you can't cut diagonally
    past a tree's corner.

  Tokens:
  - Each kind gets a default drawn icon: shield for PCs, skull for enemies,
    person for NPCs, chest for items.
  - You can pick from 20 icons when adding a token or in the token editor.

  For Claudes:
  - The text map uses one symbol per type (^ tree, * boulder, ; rubble…) and its
    legend lists the types present with their effect.
  - add_obstacles takes a type and one_object; add_token and update_token take
    an icon.
  - The lists of types and icons come straight from the model, so the GUI and
    MCP can't drift from it.

  Your map "dasd": it converts with nothing lost. Its 25 difficult squares and 1
  blocking square keep the same effects, and its 3 tokens get the default
  icons. Nothing is rewritten until you next edit it.

  Visual check: I screenshotted every type and every icon. That caught pale snow
  and a dagger that looked like the sword; both are redrawn.

  Commit? This is a new feature, not a fix, so I'd add 3 commits on top rather
  than folding into the old ones:
  1. Add obstacle types, multi-square obstacles and token icons: model, rules,
     API, MCP, text map, and their tests.
  2. Draw obstacle types and token icons in the GUI: art.js, the palette, the
     icon pickers, and the browser tests.
  3. Document obstacle types and token icons: the README.

  Terrain is now grouped by type, with theme inside:
  - Obstacles (block): Nature (tree, boulder) · Built (pillar, statue) ·
    Furniture (table, crate, barrel) · Basic (blocking).
  - Difficult terrain (costs double): Nature (undergrowth, snow) · Built
    (rubble, steep stairs) · Furniture (low furniture) · Basic (difficult).
  - Areas: deep water, chasm and lava block; shallow water, shallow bog and ice
    are difficult. Each area swatch shows its effect. Shallow water, chasm, lava
    and ice are new, and none of them model extra rules (no swimming, falling
    or fire damage).

  Painting areas: drag a rectangle to fill it, and erase single squares to shape
  the shore. Water joins seamlessly across squares, and each area gets an
  outline where it meets other ground, so a lake reads as one lake. The grid is
  now translucent, so it doesn't show white seams across lava or chasms.

  by filling a rectangle, and the text map has symbols for the new types ('
  shallow water, ? chasm, < lava, > ice).

❯ Anything else the other CLAUDE should know about?                             

⏺ Yes, a few things. Some belong in CLAUDE.md; others are just for you to pass
  on.

  Worth adding to CLAUDE.md:
  1. The installed setup runs the working copy. Your battlemap-dm MCP server and
     any running battlemap server run this checkout's files directly. The other
     Claude's half-finished edits would immediately reach your real DM
     sessions, and Python changes take effect once that server restarts.
  2. Don't touch real data. For manual testing, use another port and a temp data
     folder (BATTLEMAP_PORT=... BATTLEMAP_DATA=$(mktemp -d)). Never use the
     default 8765 or ~/.local/share/battlemap: that's your live server and your
     maps, like "dasd".
  3. What isn't modeled, so it isn't mistaken for a bug or quietly "fixed":
     - No logins.
     - No undo and no doors.
     - No squeezing into smaller spaces.
     - Tiny creatures can't share a square.
     - No swimming, falling or fire damage.
     - Radius is square-shaped by design.
     - Pathfinding speed on 200×200 maps is untested.
  4. Text-map symbols are running out. Letters and digits are reserved for
     tokens, and most punctuation is already used for terrain types. A new type
     needs a deliberate symbol choice.

  For you to tell it in chat (not repo material):
  - Pushing needs you. Your permission rules block my git push, so it will
    likely need you to run pushes with !.
  - One open offer: the selected-token editor still picks icons from a dropdown,
    not the visual picker.
  - Your personal workflow rules (commit only at your go, short answers, and so
    on) live in your own ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md, not in the repo. A Claude on this
    machine gets them automatically; one elsewhere won't.

  Should I add points 1–4 to CLAUDE.md? I'd fold them into the CLAUDE.md commit,
  which is fine as long as you haven't pushed it yet. Have you pushed f5ec9a5?
